0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle s\,\mathsf{arg}_{f,i}\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle s\,\mathsf{arg}_{f,i}\hfil$\crcr}}}
,
i.e. we are interested in the single return register
\mathsf{ret}_{f}
.

Fig.

14
shows the execution relation for
\mathsf{IMP}^{\mathrm{TC}}
specialised to single return registers.

\mpr@inferstar*[left=Assign_{1}]{#1(r\coloneqq\llbracket a\rrbracket_{s})\,r^{\prime}=v}{(r\mathrel{\mathtt{\leftarrow}}a,s)\Rightarrow_{r^{\prime}}v}\par\mpr@inferstar*[left=IfT_{1}]{s\,r\neq 0\\
(p_{1},s)\Rightarrow_{r}v}{(\mathtt{IF}\ r\ \mathtt{THEN}\ p_{1}\ \mathtt{ELSE}\ p_{2},s)\Rightarrow_{r}v}\par\mpr@inferstar*[left=Seq_{1}]{(p_{1},s)\Rightarrow s^{\prime}\\
(p_{2},s^{\prime})\Rightarrow_{r}v}{(p_{1}\mathrel{\mathtt{;}}p_{2},s)\Rightarrow_{r}v}\par\mpr@inferstar*[left=IfF_{1}]{s\,r=0\\
(p_{2},s)\Rightarrow_{r}v}{(\mathtt{IF}\ r\ \mathtt{THEN}\ p_{1}\ \mathtt{ELSE}\ p_{2},s)\Rightarrow_{r}v}\par\mpr@inferstar*[left=Call_{1}]{(pc,s)\Rightarrow_{r}v\\
#1(r\coloneqq v)\,r^{\prime}=v^{\prime}}{(\mathtt{CALL}\ pc\ \mathtt{RETURN}\ r,s)\Rightarrow_{r^{\prime}}v^{\prime}}\par\mpr@inferstar*[left=Rec_{1}]{p\vdash(p,s)\Rightarrow_{r}v}{p\vdash(\mathtt{RECURSE},s)\Rightarrow_{r}v}

Figure 14
:
Execution relation of
\mathsf{IMP}^{\mathrm{TC}}
for single return registers.

Note that for sequences
(p_{1}\mathrel{\mathtt{;}}p_{2},s)\Rightarrow_{r}v
,
the standard execution relation “
\Rightarrow
” (and not “
\Rightarrow_{r}
”) must be used for
p_{1}
,
that is
(p_{1},s)\Rightarrow s^{\prime}
.

This is the reason why each compiled
\mathsf{IMP}^{\mathrm{TC}}
program
p
is normalised such that no recursive constructor (sequences and if-then-elses)
appears on the left of a sequence.
This way, all goals involving recursive constructors use the execution relation “
\Rightarrow_{r}
”,
saving us from implementing separate recursive automation for “
\Rightarrow
”.

Appendix 0.C
\mathsf{IMP}^{\mathrm{TC}}
to
\mathsf{IMP}^{\mathrm{-}}

0.C.1
\mathsf{IMP}^{\mathrm{TC}}
to
\mathsf{IMP}^{\mathrm{WC}}

Lemma
.

\mpr@inferstar*[]{s^{\prime}=#1(r\coloneqq\llbracket a\rrbracket_{s})}{(r\mathrel{\mathtt{\leftarrow}}a,s)\Rightarrow^{\mathcal{C}}(s^{\prime},\mathtt{0})}\par\mpr@inferstar*[]{s\,r\neq 0\\
(p_{1},s)\Rightarrow^{n}(s^{\prime},b)}{(\mathtt{IF}\ r\ \mathtt{THEN}\ p_{1}\ \mathtt{ELSE}\ p_{2},s)\Rightarrow^{n+\mathcal{C}}(s^{\prime},b)}\par\mpr@inferstar*[]{(p_{1},s)\Rightarrow^{n_{1}}(s^{\prime},\mathtt{0})\\
(p_{2},s^{\prime})\Rightarrow^{n_{2}}(s^{\prime\prime},b)}{(p_{1}\mathrel{\mathtt{;}}p_{2},s)\Rightarrow^{n_{1}+n_{2}+\mathcal{C}}(s^{\prime\prime},b)}\par\mpr@inferstar*[]{s\,r=0\\
(p_{2},s)\Rightarrow^{n}(s^{\prime},b)}{(\mathtt{IF}\ r\ \mathtt{THEN}\ p_{1}\ \mathtt{ELSE}\ p_{2},s)\Rightarrow^{n+\mathcal{C}}(s^{\prime},b)}\par\mpr@inferstar*[]{(pc,s)\Rightarrow^{n}_{r}v\\
s^{\prime}=#1(r\coloneqq v)}{(\mathtt{CALL}\ pc\ \mathtt{RETURN}\ r,s)\Rightarrow^{n+\mathcal{C}}(s^{\prime},\mathtt{0})}\par\mpr@inferstar*[]{}{(\mathtt{RECURSE},s)\Rightarrow^{\mathcal{C}}(s,\mathtt{1})}

Figure 15
:
Auxiliary execution relation used in
Lemma

1
.

Let
(p,s)\Rightarrow^{n}(s^{\prime},b)

denote the modified execution relation shown in
Fig.

15
that runs

p
but stops and flags whenever a tail-recursive call is encountered using
b\in\{\mathtt{0},\mathtt{1}\}

Then the following rules are admissible:

\mpr@inferstar*[]{(p,s)\Rightarrow^{n}(s^{\prime},\mathtt{0})}{(p,s)\Rightarrow^{n}s^{\prime}}\par\mpr@inferstar*[]{(p,s_{1})\Rightarrow^{n_{1}}(s_{2},\mathtt{1})\\
tp\vdash(tp,s_{2})\Rightarrow^{n_{2}}s_{3}}{tp\vdash(p,s_{1})\Rightarrow^{n_{1}+n_{2}}s_{3}}

Proof sketch.

By induction over the execution rules.
∎

Theorem
.

Let
\mathtt{cnt}
be a fresh register with respect to
\mathsf{regs}\,\,p
.
Let
p[t/x]
denote the syntactic substitution of any occurrence of
x
by
t
in a program
p
.
Define

\displaystyle\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis_{\circlearrowleft}\equiv\mathtt{cnt}\mathrel{\mathtt{\leftarrow}}\mathtt{1}\mathrel{\mathtt{;}}\mathtt{WHILE}\ \mathtt{cnt}\ \mathtt{DO}\ \mathtt{cnt}\mathrel{\mathtt{\leftarrow}}\mathtt{0}\mathrel{\mathtt{;}}p[(\mathtt{cnt}\mathrel{\mathtt{\leftarrow}}\mathtt{1})/\mathtt{RECURSE}].

Then it holds that

p\vdash(p,s)\Rightarrow^{n}_{\mathsf{regs}\,p}s^{\prime}
if and only if
(\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis_{\circlearrowleft},s)\Rightarrow^{n+\mathcal{C}}_{\mathsf{regs}\,p}s^{\prime}
.

Proof sketch.

We show that

\vdash(p,s)\Rightarrow^{n}(s^{\prime},b)
holds
if and only if

\exists s^{\prime\prime}.\,(p[(\mathtt{cnt}\mathrel{\mathtt{\leftarrow}}\mathtt{1})/\mathtt{RECURSE}],s)\Rightarrow^{n}s^{\prime\prime}\land s^{\prime\prime}\,\mathtt{cnt}=b\land\forall r\in\mathsf{regs}\,p.\,s^{\prime}\,p=s^{\prime\prime}\,p

by induction over the execution rules, generalising over
S

such that
\mathsf{regs}\,p\subseteq S
and
\mathtt{cnt}\notin S
.
Then the semantics for the while-loop with rules from
Fig.

9
are equivalent to the semantics of the compiled program with rules from
Lemma

1
by straightforward induction over the respective rules.
∎

0.C.2
\mathsf{IMP}^{\mathrm{WC}}
to
\mathsf{IMP}^{\mathrm{W}}

Lemma
.

(p[m\,x/x],s)\Rightarrow^{n}s^{\prime}
implies
(p,s\circ m)\Rightarrow^{n}s^{\prime}\circ m

for injective
m
.

Proof sketch.

By induction over the execution of
p
.
∎

The inlining induces a blow-up that is dependent on the program size (but not the input), as shown below.

Theorem
.

(p,s)\Rightarrow^{n}_{\mathsf{regs}\,\,p}s^{\prime}
if and only if
(\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis_{\star},s)\Rightarrow^{f(n)}_{\mathsf{regs}\,\,p}s^{\prime}
,
where
f(n)
is linear in
n\cdot\lvert p\rvert
.

Proof sketch.

The inlining is correct by
Lemma

2
, so correctness follows by a simple induction.
∎

0.C.3
\mathsf{IMP}^{\mathrm{W}}
to
\mathsf{IMP}^{\mathrm{-}}

Lemma
.

Assume that
\text{max}\,\{a_{\text{max}},\llbracket a\rrbracket_{s},s_{\text{max}}\}<2^{w}
.
Then it holds that

({\llparenthesis a\rrparenthesis}^{w}_{r},{\llparenthesis s\rrparenthesis}^{w})\Rightarrow^{f(w)}{\llparenthesis#1(r\coloneqq\llbracket a\rrbracket_{s})\rrparenthesis}^{w}
,
where
f(w)
is linear in
w
.

Proof Sketch.

The proof is based on showing an upper bound on the running time of each of the functions
\mathsf{copy}^{a}\,b\,y
and
\mathsf{adder}^{w}\,v
, which in turn depends on the running time of
\mathsf{fullAdder}^{i}\,r
.
The running time of
\mathsf{fullAdder}^{i}\,r
is a constant.
The running times of
\mathsf{adder}\,
and
\mathsf{copy}\,\,
are shown to be linear in
w
by induction on
w
.
∎

-Definitions

shows the complete specification of

the rule for
\mathtt{WHILE}
, and

Fig.

18
the complete specification of
\mathsf{IMP}^{\mathrm{-}}
.

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathtt{C}\,n\rrbracket_{s}\equiv n,\hskip 8.99994pt\llbracket\mathtt{R}\,r\rrbracket_{s}\equiv s\,r

(a)

Evaluation of atoms
A
.

\displaystyle\llbracket A_{1}\otimes A_{2}\rrbracket_{s}\equiv\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket_{s}\otimes\llbracket A_{2}\rrbracket_{s},\text{ for }\otimes\in\{+,-\}

(b)

Evaluation rules for arithmetic expressions.

\mpr@inferstar*[left=Assign]{s^{\prime}=#1(r\coloneqq\llbracket a\rrbracket_{s})}{(r\mathrel{\mathtt{\leftarrow}}a,s)\Rightarrow^{\mathcal{C}}s^{\prime}}\par\mpr@inferstar*[left=IfT]{s\,r\neq 0\\
(p_{1},s)\Rightarrow^{n}s^{\prime}}{(\mathtt{IF}\ r\ \mathtt{THEN}\ p_{1}\ \mathtt{ELSE}\ p_{2},s)\Rightarrow^{n+\mathcal{C}}s^{\prime}}\par\mpr@inferstar*[left=Seq]{(p_{1},s)\Rightarrow^{n_{1}}s^{\prime}\\
(p_{2},s^{\prime})\Rightarrow^{n_{2}}s^{\prime\prime}}{(p_{1}\mathrel{\mathtt{;}}p_{2},s)\Rightarrow^{n_{1}+n_{2}+\mathcal{C}}s^{\prime\prime}}\par\mpr@inferstar*[left=IfF]{s\,r=0\\
(p_{2},s)\Rightarrow^{n}s^{\prime}}{(\mathtt{IF}\ r\ \mathtt{THEN}\ p_{1}\ \mathtt{ELSE}\ p_{2},s)\Rightarrow^{n+\mathcal{C}}s^{\prime}}\par\mpr@inferstar*[left=Call]{(pc,s)\Rightarrow^{n}_{r}v\\
s^{\prime}=#1(r\coloneqq v)}{(\mathtt{CALL}\ pc\ \mathtt{RETURN}\ r,s)\Rightarrow^{n}s^{\prime}}\par\mpr@inferstar*[left=Rec]{p\vdash(p,s)\Rightarrow^{n}s^{\prime}}{p\vdash(\mathtt{RECURSE},s)\Rightarrow^{n+\mathcal{C}}s^{\prime}}

:
Semantics of

(p,s_{1})\Rightarrow^{n_{1}}s_{2}\\
(\mathtt{WHILE}\ r\ \mathtt{DO}\ p,s_{2})\Rightarrow^{n_{2}}s_{3}}{(\mathtt{WHILE}\ r\ \mathtt{DO}\ p,s_{1})\Rightarrow^{n_{1}+n_{2}+\mathcal{C}}s_{3}}

\mpr@inferstar*[]{b\in\{\mathtt{0},\mathtt{1}\}\\
s^{\prime}=#1(r\coloneqq b)}{(r\mathrel{\mathtt{\leftarrow}}b,s)\Rightarrow^{1}s^{\prime}}\par\mpr@inferstar*[]{s\,r\neq\mathtt{0}\\
(p_{1},s)\Rightarrow^{n}s^{\prime}}{(\mathtt{IF}\ r\ \mathtt{THEN}\ p_{1}\ \mathtt{ELSE}\ p_{2},s)\Rightarrow^{n+1}s^{\prime}}\par\mpr@inferstar*[]{(p_{1},s)\Rightarrow^{n_{1}}s^{\prime}\\

\mathsf{IMP}^{\mathrm{-}}
.
